([yannickloth/claude-router-system](https://github.com/yannickloth/claude-router-system/blob/HEAD/plugins/infolead-claude-subscription-router/agents/opus-general.md))
- **opus-general-purpose**: An unprompted generic subagent. Uses Opus. Intended for tasks that require deep reasoning, complex analysis, or nuanced judgment. ([ed3dai/ed3d-plugins](https://github.com/ed3dai/ed3d-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/ed3d-basic-agents/agents/opus-general-purpose.md) · ★253 · 2 copies)
- **opus-general-purpose**: Heavyweight subagent for tasks requiring deep reasoning, complex analysis, or nuanced judgment. Good for architectural decisions, difficult debugging, and tasks where quality is paramount. Use sparingly due to cost. ([brmatola/dotfiles](https://github.com/brmatola/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/claude/agents/opus-general-purpose.md) · 2 copies)
- **opus-heavy-implementer**: Heavy implementation lane running Claude Opus 5 (high) for complex algorithms, concurrency, data migrations, security-sensitive code, and changes spanning many files. Outside the luna/grok tally. Receives the architect's full spec; implements, verifies with real evidence, returns OPUS-HEAVY REPORT; its diff is reviewed by codex-peer. ([ricardosuman/maestro](https://github.com/ricardosuman/maestro/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-heavy-implementer.md))
- **opus-high**: Verification and edge-case agent (Opus at high effort). Use for security, bugs whose cause is unknown, concurrency, storage and low-level code, parsers, reviews of that kind of code, and a retry after opus-medium under-delivered or a thin medium review. ([ychampion/ystack](https://github.com/ychampion/ystack/blob/HEAD/claude/agents/opus-high.md) · ★5)
- **opus-high**: Internal Kerd routing agent for Opus at high reasoning effort. Invoke only when Kerd Conductor or Agent explicitly selects kerd:opus-high for a delegated job; do not choose it for ordinary requests. The name carries the model and the effort so the running-job list shows both. The dispatching call still names the model (opus); this definition sets the same model, so a call that omits it still resolves to Opus, unless the host forces or restricts the subagent model. ([anthonymaley/Kerd](https://github.com/anthonymaley/Kerd/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-high.md) · ★3)
- **opus-high**: Default subagent seat and the floor: Opus at effort high. Every assignment that is not clearly heavy — searches, reading and analysis, docs, merges, fix rounds, implementing a decided design, small features, small mockup changes. In doubt, this one. ([fo0/listen-to-me](https://github.com/fo0/listen-to-me/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high.md) · ★3 · 5 copies)
- **opus-high**: Worker for the hardest tasks — genuinely novel architectural/design judgment with no existing pattern to extend, or a task that has already failed twice at sonnet-high in the fix loop. A normal dispatch tier, distinct from opus-review's read-only second-opinion pass. ([dariovr1/sixth-sense](https://github.com/dariovr1/sixth-sense/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-high.md) · ★1)
- **opus-high**: General-purpose worker running the latest Opus model at high reasoning effort. Use ONLY when the user explicitly asks for the "opus-high" subagent (or "both" subagents) in their current message; the user will state the task. Never spawn it on your own initiative. ([RishiJain905/rlm-harness](https://github.com/RishiJain905/rlm-harness/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high.md) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **opus-high**: Generic worker pinned to model opus at effort high. Use only when explicitly dispatching per the superpowers-dispatch policy — never auto-select. ([UmutEmreOnder/superpowers-dispatch](https://github.com/UmutEmreOnder/superpowers-dispatch/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-high.md) · ★1)
- **opus-high**: Delegated worker (Opus) at high effort, for work where judgment decides the outcome: architecture, security-sensitive or cross-cutting review, concurrency, hard migrations, multi-system debugging, long-horizon runs, or a task a Sonnet worker had the context for and still got wrong. ([bantoilatoi/AgentSkills](https://github.com/bantoilatoi/AgentSkills/blob/HEAD/setup/claude/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: Default orchestration delegate pinned to Claude Opus at high reasoning effort. Use for frontend implementation, artifacts, mock-ups, HTML pages, prompt text, and code review. Escalate to opus-xhigh only when a task earns it. ([Chasonnnn/agentic-engineering-skills-personal](https://github.com/Chasonnnn/agentic-engineering-skills-personal/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: General-purpose agent pinned to opus-5 at high reasoning effort. No role framing. ([Coding-downunder/ai-simply](https://github.com/Coding-downunder/ai-simply/blob/HEAD/operating-system/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: The research workhorse — Opus 5 leads agentic search and computer/tool work. Excellent for sweeps, fact-checks, and verification passes. Faster still. ([david-lagrange/alpaca-mind](https://github.com/david-lagrange/alpaca-mind/blob/HEAD/mission/.claude/agents/opus-high.md) · 2 copies)
- **opus-high**: Opus at high reasoning effort. Sub-agent for judgement-heavy work (code review axes, verifiers, fix rounds). Never Fable. ([lazycoder9/three-sixes](https://github.com/lazycoder9/three-sixes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: Router worker: Opus 5.5 at high effort. Started only by the router. ([Light505/gswitchtool](https://github.com/Light505/gswitchtool/blob/HEAD/integrations/router-plugin/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: Fixed-tier executor (claude-opus-5-5, high effort). Use per the global routing matrix — never inherits the orchestrator's model. ([rwspatin/agent-orchestration](https://github.com/rwspatin/agent-orchestration/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high**: General-purpose worker on Opus 5.5 at high effort, for the harder plan tasks (integration across files, engine wiring) and their reviews. ([wmaq/wmaq-claude-mods](https://github.com/wmaq/wmaq-claude-mods/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high.md))
- **opus-high-implementer**: Implements exactly one ticket end to end (TDD at the agreed seam, fmt, clippy, tests) and reports raw verification data. Used by the /implement flow; one fresh instance per ticket, resumed for fix rounds. ([ocn/Picket](https://github.com/ocn/Picket/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high-implementer.md) · ★7)
- **opus-high-reviewer**: Sole code reviewer for one ticket along Standards and Spec axes; strictly read-only; verifies claims by reading code and running tests; returns ranked findings and a verdict. ([ocn/Picket](https://github.com/ocn/Picket/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-high-reviewer.md) · ★7)
- **opus-highlevel-dev**: Development agent for open-ended work (opus). Use when the parent agent knows the goal but not the change: the agent must find the cause or choose the approach. Typical tasks: diagnose a bug with an unknown cause, design and implement a change across modules, refactor with design choices, analyze tradeoffs, draft a plan or ADR. ([HernandoR/agent-skillset](https://github.com/HernandoR/agent-skillset/blob/HEAD/plugins/subagents/agents/opus-highlevel-dev.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Claude-side implementer for the delegate skill. Types code from a written spec that the main session already decided. Runs Claude Opus 5.5 at medium effort. Use only through the delegate skill, with a complete spec. ([pvjbuilds/delegate-router](https://github.com/pvjbuilds/delegate-router/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md) · ★42)
- **opus-implementer**: A Variorum batch IMPLEMENTER — Opus at xhigh effort, one batch (one acceptance unit, one surface) per session, briefed by a plan file's named sections. Spawn one at a time, never concurrently with another agent. The author's standing cast since 2026-09-07 — Opus builds, Fable orchestrates and cold-reviews. ([lduarte-io/Variorum](https://github.com/lduarte-io/Variorum/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer.md) · ★16)
- **opus-implementer**: Executes judgment-heavy implementation tasks. Best for architectural choices, multi-file refactors with system-wide coupling not anticipated in the plan, ambiguous requirements, cross-cutting debugging, and design synthesis. This is the highest tier — there is no escalation above Opus. Accept the task and produce your best output even when the brief is incomplete. ([davistroy/claude-marketplace](https://github.com/davistroy/claude-marketplace/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer.md) · ★5)
- **opus-implementer**: Implements a plan. Code edits, typecheck, lint, tests, git, and outward actions (push, PR, issue tracker, chat) only on the user's explicit command. ([madvil2/devkit](https://github.com/madvil2/devkit/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md) · ★2)
- **opus-implementer**: Implements judged and hard work on opus-5.5 — features, refactors, anything user-facing (UI, copy, API design), and anything genuinely difficult. The orchestrator states the effort level: medium for routine, high for hard, max for very hard or taste-critical work. For mechanical, repetitive, clear-spec volume work where free execution is the point, use the sol-implementer instead. Not for planning, architecture decisions, or review. ([chrfalch/ledger](https://github.com/chrfalch/ledger/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer**: Use this agent for well-specified implementation work that needs real engineering judgment but not top-tier reasoning. Typical triggers include multi-file features with a defined interface, refactors backed by tests, bug fixes where the root cause is already known, and writing tests for existing behavior. Runs on Opus. See "When to invoke" in the agent body. ([Brainwires/fable-lite](https://github.com/Brainwires/fable-lite/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Opus 5 implementation worker. The Fable 5 orchestrator plans and reviews; this agent does the hands-on coding — features, refactors, UI builds, tools/scrapers, tests, debugging — following a task brief. Use it for any substantial implementation work to keep Fable 5 token spend low. ([DianCotrina/keikogobierna](https://github.com/DianCotrina/keikogobierna/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Sweep implementer pinned to cc/claude-opus-5[1m]. ESCALATION TIER — dispatch requires a recorded operator checkpoint. For: escalation only — design work, cross-surface doctrine, anything with a real judgment call the spec does not already settle. Fallback when cc/claude-opus-5[1m] is unavailable in the subscription: cc/claude-opus-4-8[1m] (record which model actually served). ([evanstern/kithcraft](https://github.com/evanstern/kithcraft/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer.md) · 2 copies)
- **opus-implementer**: Implementation delegate for hard slices (Opus, high effort). Writes and edits code from a detailed brief prepared by the session architect. Chosen up front for work whose difficulty is in the reasoning, never as a retry after a smaller model failed. ([LivinTribunal/architect-skill](https://github.com/LivinTribunal/architect-skill/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md) · 2 copies)
- **opus-implementer**: Claude implementation lane on Opus 5.5 with edit tools. Route hard work here when judgment the spec cannot capture decides the outcome and turnaround matters more than cross-vendor diversity, or to race sol-implementer on a high-stakes change. Spends the Claude subscription. Receives the six-part spec, implements directly, runs the spec's verification command, returns a LANE REPORT with observed changes. Never widens scope, never commits. ([mhrtly/astrafabulopus](https://github.com/mhrtly/astrafabulopus/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Opus implementation agent at xhigh effort — the standard workhorse for well-specified demo↔phone parity work packages (feature/UI/logic) where the phase-review pipeline provides the safety net. Use for M-sized packages of moderate subtlety. ([Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes](https://github.com/Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Fallback implementation lane running Claude Opus 5 (medium) — used when luna-lane or grok-lane report unavailable/rate-limited/timeout, or on explicit request. Receives the architect's full five-part spec; implements, verifies with real evidence, returns OPUS REPORT. Its diff is reviewed by codex-peer, not opus-reviewer. ([ricardosuman/maestro](https://github.com/ricardosuman/maestro/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer.md))
- **opus-implementer**: Independent implementation agent for sustained multi-file coding, complex refactors, and a provider-diverse second execution path. Use Claude Opus when a task benefits from careful long-horizon tool use. ([tarushnagpal/config](https://github.com/tarushnagpal/config/blob/HEAD/pi/agents/opus-implementer.md))

- **opus-implementer-high**: Deep-reasoning implementation lane running Claude Opus at HIGH reasoning effort. Route correctness-critical work here — concurrency, security-sensitive code, data migrations, algorithms, or anything costly to revert. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. Same model family as the architect — use the grok lane when you want vendor diversity. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-high.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer-high**: Opus implementation agent at high effort — the quota-friendly tier for S-sized, well-specified, mechanical parity packages (copy ports, option lists, small advisory branches, UI stubs). Same conventions as opus-implementer, leaner reasoning budget. ([Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes](https://github.com/Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer-high.md))
- **opus-implementer-low**: Implementation lane running Claude Opus at LOW reasoning effort. Route mechanical or boilerplate changes here where the spec fully determines the output — but prefer the grok lane for this class of work unless you specifically want a Claude producer. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-low.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer-max**: Opus implementation agent at MAX effort — for the subtler parity work packages assigned to Opus (nuanced UX ports, tricky state interactions, packages whose failure modes reviews catch late). Same conventions as opus-implementer, deeper reasoning budget. ([Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes](https://github.com/Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer-max.md))
- **opus-implementer-medium**: Implementation lane running Claude Opus at MEDIUM reasoning effort. Route non-trivial logic that isn't safety-critical here — standard feature code, moderate refactors. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. Same model family as the architect — use the grok lane when you want vendor diversity. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-medium.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer-xhigh**: Heavy implementation delegate (Opus, xhigh effort). ([LivinTribunal/architect-skill](https://github.com/LivinTribunal/architect-skill/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-xhigh.md) · 2 copies)
